All four counter-affecting mutations (`setCompleted`/`softDeleteItem`/`restoreItem`/`deleteItem`) run inside a real Firestore transaction via a new **synchronous cross-boundary transaction-callback pattern**: a pure Kotlin `decide: (Map<String, FirebaseValue>?) -> ItemCounterOutcome` callback is invoked from inside Swift's real `firestore.runTransaction` `updateBlock`, keeping all counter policy testable in Kotlin while Swift only executes SDK transaction mechanics — reviewer ruled this sound (non-suspending, idempotent-by-construction under transaction retry, safe under Kotlin/Native's new memory model) and corroborated it with a live 5-concurrent-coroutine `setCompleted` race converging correctly under the real emulator. Counters never route through `FieldPatch` (`ItemCounterOutcome.counterDelta: Map<String, FirebaseValue.Number>` only). `clearCompleted` uses the same chunked/resumable query-driven loop shape as `FB-204`. Reuses `com.fluxit.firebase.list` iosMain internals unmodified (`CurrentUidProvider`, `toListRepositoryException()`/`ListRepositoryException`, `IosFirestoreListDocument` typealias, `IosFirestoreListenerHandle`) — confirmed empty diff on that package.
- **A fourth iOS/Kotlin-Native interop bug found and fixed this round (beyond `FB-203`'s three), carried forward as a standing fact for any future bridge work:** a Kotlin primitive (e.g. `Int`) crosses **unboxed** into Swift as a plain method parameter, but the *same* primitive crosses **boxed** (`KotlinInt`/`ComposeAppInt`) when it appears inside a Kotlin function-type/closure parameter (e.g. `(Int, NSError?) -> Unit`) — discovered from an initial protocol-conformance build failure and confirmed from the generated `ComposeApp.h` header; fixed by declaring the Swift completion as `(KotlinInt, Error?) -> Void`. The reviewer states they read `ComposeApp.h` directly (not merely asserted the fix); this handoff pass did not re-derive the header inspection itself but treats it as reviewer-corroborated. Recorded as `FB-205-NB1` (see below), now present in the follow-ups table.

- **What `FB-105` did, precisely (do not re-derive from the ledger prose alone — this is the load-bearing summary):**
  - **`DEC-006`** (recorded 2026-09-18, before delegation) settled `FB-104-NB2`: the gate now times out a hung initial `restoreSession()` after **10 seconds**, falling back to the signed-out auth screen with a non-alarming "could not restore your session" notice plus a retry affordance — never an error screen, never `ResolutionFailed`. Implemented as `SessionGateViewModel.InitialRestorationTimeout = 10.seconds`, a named, retunable companion constant. Reviewer verified the fallback holds even when a cached `Authenticated` session was already reported (timing out is not permission to trust an unvalidated credential). Covered by 6 automated virtual-time tests (the suite never sleeps) — **not exercised manually on a device this round.**
  - **Headline correctness result — the `key(uid)` teardown assumption carried since `FB-104` was FALSE.** Reading `navigation3-runtime`/`lifecycle-viewmodel-navigation3` sources directly showed `rememberViewModelStoreNavEntryDecorator()` defaults to the root `ViewModelStoreOwner`, and per-entry stores are keyed by `NavEntry.contentKey` — `DashboardRoute` is a singleton `data object`, so that key never changes across users, and disposing a composition (all `key()` does) pops nothing from the back stack. Two real leaks resulted: (1) user B was handed user A's `DashboardViewModel` instance; (2) the auth form's email field carried A's text into B's sign-in. Both reproduced on-device before the fix.
  - **Fix:** a new teardown boundary, `SessionScopedViewModelStores.kt` (125 lines, new), a root `ViewModel` handing out a fresh `ViewModelStore` per `SessionScope` (`SignedOut` | `User(uid)`), provided as `LocalViewModelStoreOwner` by the gate, clearing the outgoing store on every scope change. Signed-out is a first-class scope, so the auth form gets the same boundary (the developer explicitly rejected an alternative of making `AuthViewModel` self-reset by observing `session`, since that adds a second app-lifetime SDK-listener collection). `key(uid)` is retained but now understood correctly — it only resets the back stack, not any `ViewModelStore`.
  - **`DEC-003a` honored as scoped:** `signOut()` stays Auth-only; no Firestore/Storage SDK surface added (both adapters' stale "FB-105's job" KDoc corrected to name the real deferral — Phase 2 Firestore / Phase 3 Storage — comment-only diffs, reviewer-confirmed zero functional change).
  - **`FB-104-NB1` (the `yield()` residual race) narrowed, not closed:** restoration now awaits a resolved `latestSession` bounded by the same 10s `DEC-006` budget. A provable fix still needs an `AuthRepository.restoreSession()` contract change, deliberately out of scope again (awaiting a *new* emission would stall every ordinary cold start for the full 10s, since both adapters' `distinctUntilChanged` means a confirming restore emits nothing). Stays genuinely `OPEN`.
- **What `FB-106` must state explicitly — `FB-105-NB1`, the single most important carry-forward from this round.** Phase 1's exit criterion text ("handles session changes without leaking data between users") is **literally broader** than what `FB-105` could achieve. After an A->B switch, B still sees A's seeded Room lists: the pre-existing Room store is one undifferentiated local database with **no per-uid partition at all** (reviewer confirmed by direct reading — `ListRepository`/`ItemRepository`/`DebugSeeder` carry no `uid` parameter anywhere; Room predates the auth system entirely). Partitioning Room inside Phase 1 would be scope creep and throwaway work (Room is slated for deletion in Phase 7). **Ruling: not blocking for `FB-105`.** But `FB-106` **must record explicitly** that the "no leaking data between users" criterion is satisfied only for **auth-derived in-memory state and listeners** (the `SessionScopedViewModelStores` fix, the app-scoped auth listener disposal), with full data isolation deferred to **Phase 2** (`FB-201`+, Firestore `users/{uid}` scoping) and **Phase 7** (Room removal) — not silently assumed complete.

A **LAN** emulator host (not `127.0.0.1`) would likely need `NSAllowsLocalNetworking`, Debug-scoped — out of scope today. A Kotlin `suspend` function cannot be implemented from Swift, hence the bridge uses completion handlers converted back via `suspendCancellableCoroutine` on the Kotlin side. Kotlin's `(NSError?) -> Unit` parameter type imports into Swift as `((any Error)?) -> Void` — expect this exact signature shape for any future Kotlin/Swift completion-handler bridge. `xcrun simctl launch` on an **already-running** app does **not** re-run `AppDelegate` — the app must be terminated between launches for any `AppDelegate`-driven state to actually re-execute.
  - **Open `FB-000` item, still not moved:** Koin starts in `MainViewController.kt:11-17` (shared `commonMain`/Compose entry point, unchanged this pass — `startKoin { modules(platformModule(), appModule) }` guarded by a `koinStarted` flag), while `FirebaseApp.configure()` runs earlier in `AppDelegate.didFinishLaunchingWithOptions` on iOS. ## Status values

`NOT_STARTED`, `READY`, `IN_PROGRESS`, `IN_REVIEW`, `CHANGES_REQUESTED`, `VERIFIED`, `DONE`, `BLOCKED_USER`, `BLOCKED_TECHNICAL`, `SKIPPED`.

Manual actions use `NOT_STARTED`, `REQUESTED`, `PASS`, `WAIVED`, or `FAILED`. Only `PASS` and a user-approved `WAIVED` satisfy a manual gate. `MAN-*` IDs are linked evidence records, not task dependencies.

Interactive-operation failures return `AuthResult.Failure` and leave session state unchanged, so a wrong password does not masquerade as a broken session. `AuthError` = `InvalidCredentials`/`InvalidEmail`/`EmailAlreadyInUse`/`WeakPassword`/`UserNotFound`/`UserDisabled`/`TooManyRequests`/`NetworkUnavailable`/`SessionExpired`/`Unknown`. **Constraints verified by the reviewer, not merely claimed:** Firebase-neutral (`grep -rni 'firebase|com.google'` over all four files -> 0 matches; `git diff` over `gradle/`, `composeApp/build.gradle.kts`, `build.gradle.kts`, `settings.gradle.kts` -> empty, no new dependency); `DEC-001` email+password only, no provider argument; `PLAN-008` iOS-adapter shape sound (all `suspend` over immutable sealed types, one `Flow` of immutable values, no generics or platform types crossing the boundary); `DEC-003a` not contradicted (`signOut` KDoc documents cache teardown explicitly); typed taxonomy with no raw SDK code/string in the commonMain public surface; no regression (Room, ViewModels, navigation, DI, Gradle, Rules, iOS sources all byte-unchanged — only a forward-looking doc comment at `AndroidFirebaseInitializer.kt:19` even mentions these types). **Evidence (`PLAN-004` satisfied — forced, and XML timestamps read from disk by BOTH developer and reviewer independently):** developer ran `./gradlew :composeApp:testDebugUnitTest :composeApp:testReleaseUnitTest :composeApp:iosSimulatorArm64Test --rerun-tasks` at 14:20:35Z-14:20:50Z -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, 73 actionable / **73 executed, 0 UP-TO-DATE**; reviewer **independently re-ran the same command** at 14:22:26Z -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, 73/73 executed, and read all **18 JUnit XML files** directly, confirming XML `timestamp` attributes (Android `14:22:33Z`, iOS `14:22:42Z`) against the reviewer's own `date -u` (`14:22:46Z`). | FB-102 | DONE | Developer/implementation | Implement Android official Auth adapter. | FB-101 | Android integration checks for sign-up/in/recovery/sign-out/restoration. | 2026-09-18. **Reviewer `APPROVED` round 1, no blocking findings**, all evidence independently re-run. **Added:** `composeApp/src/androidMain/kotlin/com/fluxit/firebase/auth/AndroidAuthRepository.kt` (the adapter), `FirebaseAuthGateway.kt` (internal `FirebaseSdkAuthGateway` seam + `AuthStateRegistration` + a `Task`->`suspend` bridge, no new coroutines-play-services dep), `FirebaseAuthErrorMapper.kt` (`AuthDiagnostics`/`LogcatAuthDiagnostics`, pure `authErrorForCode`, `mapAuthFailure`); `composeApp/src/androidUnitTest/.../{AndroidAuthRepositoryTest(17),RecordingAuthGateway,FirebaseAuthErrorMapperTest(4)}`; `composeApp/src/androidInstrumentedTest/.../{FirebaseAuthEmulatorIntegrationTest(13),AndroidAuthDiInstrumentedTest(2)}`; `composeApp/src/debug/AndroidManifest.xml` + `src/debug/res/xml/network_security_config.xml`. **Modified (3 tracked, +23):** `di/PlatformModule.android.kt` (+9, `single<AuthRepository> { AndroidAuthRepository() }`), `composeApp/build.gradle.kts` (+10, `androidInstrumentedTest` deps + `AndroidJUnitRunner`), `gradle/libs.versions.toml` (+4). **No new production dependency** — `firebase-auth` was already an `androidMain` dep from `FB-006`. **FB-101 contract byte-unchanged**, independently re-verified (`git diff HEAD -- commonMain/.../domain/auth/ commonTest/` -> empty). **Design:** `session` is a `callbackFlow` that sends current state first (deterministically `Unresolved` until resolved — `Unresolved`/`SignedOut` stay distinct for `FB-104`'s gate), registers the SDK `AuthStateListener`, relays a private `MutableStateFlow`, `awaitClose { registration.remove(); relay.cancel() }`, `.distinctUntilChanged()`. State lives in the StateFlow, not the flow, so operations with no collector still leave correct state and `restoreSession()` can publish `ResolutionFailed` (a state the SDK listener can never report). `restoreSession()` calls `reload()` against the server for a persisted user, so a revoked/disabled/deleted credential does not pass silently. **`FB-101-NB2` discharged and reviewer-verified at `FirebaseAuthErrorMapper.kt:93-108`:** `mapAuthFailure` calls `diagnostics.onSdkFailure(...)` **before** returning `mapped ?: Unknown`, on every call site; unmapped failures log at `Log.e` with the full throwable and an explicit UNMAPPED marker. Asserted non-trivially by `anUnmappedSdkFailureIsLoggedBeforeItBecomesUnknown` (`assertSame(original, logged.throwable)`). This logging proved its own worth mid-task: the first instrumented run failed with an otherwise-opaque `Unknown`, and the log line was the only thing identifying the cause (blocked cleartext to `10.0.2.2`). **`FB-101-NB3` discharged — reviewer judged this the strongest part of the deliverable and explicitly non-vacuous.** Unit: `aReleasedListenerStopsReceivingUpdatesAndDoesNotResurrectTheCollector` asserts `removeCount==1` + `liveListeners.isEmpty()` **and then** emits a further auth-state change and asserts the emission count did not grow (proves the listener is functionally dead, not just that a counter moved); `cancellingTheCollectorRemovesTheUnderlyingAuthStateListener` (add=1/remove=1), `eachCollectorOwnsItsOwnListenerAndReleasesIt` (2/2). Binding inputs recorded before work started: (a) **`DEC-006`** settles `FB-104-NB2` — the gate gets a 10s timeout on the initial `restoreSession()` falling back to signed-out + retry, so this task must not inherit that gap silently; (b) **`DEC-003a`** requires sign-out to clear the Firebase local persistent cache, but `FB-105`'s `signOut()` scope is **Auth-only** — Firestore/Storage `terminate()`/`clearPersistence()` stay deferred because neither SDK is in any data path yet (reviewer-confirmed correct as scoped by `FB-102`/`FB-103`, not an under-delivery); (c) **`FB-104-NB1`** (the `yield()` residual race) should be narrowed if it can be done without redesign, and explicitly re-stated if not; (d) the **`DashboardViewModel`-survives-user-change** mechanism behind `SessionGate`'s `key(state.user.uid)` is an *unverified* assumption and must be proven or fixed, not assumed; (e) **auth form state** (previous user's email in `AuthViewModel`, bound at the root outside `key(uid)`) is suspected to survive a sign-out/sign-in cycle and must be checked; (f) **`PLAN-004`** evidence rule applies — force every cited test task and verify JUnit XML timestamps. Environment verified up by the orchestrator at selection: Auth emulator PID `7163` listening on `127.0.0.1:9099`, AVD booted as `emulator-5554`, iOS simulator `iPhone 17` Booted.  **Reviewer `APPROVED` round 1 (2026-09-18), zero blocking findings**, three non-blocking follow-ups (`FB-105-NB1`, `FB-105-NB2`, `FB-105-NB3`). **Headline correctness result: the `key(uid)` teardown assumption carried in the tracker since `FB-104` was FALSE, and it was masking two real leaks.** Mechanism found by reading `navigation3-runtime 1.1.1` / `lifecycle-viewmodel-navigation3 2.10.0` sources: `rememberViewModelStoreNavEntryDecorator()` defaults to `LocalViewModelStoreOwner.current` (the Activity at root); per-entry stores live in an `EntryViewModel` keyed by `NavEntry.contentKey`; `DashboardRoute` is a `data object`, so that key is IDENTICAL for every user; those stores clear only from `NavEntryDecorator.onPop`, which fires only when a content key leaves the back stack — and disposing a composition (all `key()` does) pops nothing. **Leak 1:** user B was handed user A's `DashboardViewModel` instance. **Leak 2:** the auth form retained A's email into B's sign-in. Both reproduced on-device before the fix (`and-fb105-06-negative-check-fb104-behaviour.log` shows `DashboardViewModel created` exactly once across an A->B switch; `and-fb105-07-negcheck-form-leak.png` shows the concatenated `usera@fluxit.testuserb@fluxit.test`). **Fix:** one new teardown boundary, `SessionScopedViewModelStores` (125), a root `ViewModel` handing out a fresh `ViewModelStore` per `SessionScope` (`SignedOut` | `User(uid)`) and clearing the outgoing one, provided by the gate as `LocalViewModelStoreOwner`; `key(uid)` retained, but only for its real effect (back-stack reset). Signed-out is a first-class scope, so the auth form gets the same boundary — the developer explicitly rejected making `AuthViewModel` observe `session` to self-reset, since that would add a second app-lifetime collection of the repository flow (i.e. a second SDK listener). **`DEC-006` implemented:** `SessionGateViewModel.InitialRestorationTimeout = 10.seconds`, a named companion constant, KDoc'd as retunable without reopening the decision; on expiry -> `SignedOut` + non-alarming notice + "Try again", never `ResolutionFailed` and never an error screen; reviewer verified via `publishGateState` precedence AND a passing test that the fallback holds **even when a cached `Authenticated` session was already reported** — timing out is not permission to trust an unvalidated credential. 6 tests, all virtual time; the suite never sleeps. **`DEC-003a` honored as scoped:** `signOut()` stays Auth-only, no Firestore/Storage SDK surface added; both adapters' stale "FB-105's job" KDoc corrected to name the real deferral (Phase 2 Firestore / Phase 3 Storage, with `FB-404` owning the loading-state consequence) — comment-only diffs, reviewer-confirmed zero functional change. **Deliverable:** 2 new files (`feature/auth/SessionScopedViewModelStores.kt` 125, `commonTest/SessionLifecycleTests.kt` 631, 16 `@Test` in 4 classes) + 6 modified tracked files (`SessionGateViewModel.kt` now 324, `SessionGate.kt` 224, `AuthScreen.kt` 369, `AppModule.kt`, `strings.xml`, plus the 2 KDoc-only adapter files); `git diff --stat` **7 product files, +249/-48** (orchestrator-corrected at commit time; the earlier "8 files, +252/-50" figure counted `FIREBASE_MIGRATION_STATUS.md` itself, whose diff necessarily kept growing while the deliverable sat uncommitted — the handoff pass caught this. Per-file product figures were unaffected; `git show --stat` on the commit below is the authoritative reference). All line counts orchestrator-verified against `wc -l`. **Evidence — reviewer-reproduced:** forced `:composeApp:{testDebugUnitTest,testReleaseUnitTest,iosSimulatorArm64Test} --rerun-tasks` -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, 73/73 tasks executed (not `UP-TO-DATE`), on-disk JUnit XML parsed directly = **93 / 93 / 97, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped**, XML mtimes `14:13` checked against the reviewer's own wall clock (`PLAN-004` satisfied). Baseline `FB-104` round 2 was 77/77/81, so **+16 on every target**, matching `grep -c "@Test"` = 16 in the one new test file. Orchestrator independently parsed the same XML and got the same 93/93/97. Also reviewer-reproduced: the isolation `git diff --stat` sweep (empty over `iosApp/`, `*.pbxproj`, `gradle.properties`, `gradle/libs.versions.toml`, `firebase/`, `AuthModels.kt`, `AuthRepository.kt`, `composeApp/build.gradle.kts`, `MainViewController.kt`), the neutrality grep (exactly 3 hits, all KDoc prose, zero imports/types), `git check-ignore -v` on both mobile config files, and direct source reading. **Three negative checks, all bit and all reverted:** store-always-reused mutation -> 5 failures; `withTimeoutOrNull` removed -> 5 failures (reviewer inspected the real Gradle `FAILED` output and diffed the `.bak` snapshots against current source, `diff` exit 0 on all three, confirming clean reversion); device-level `FB-104`-behaviour revert -> both leaks reproduced. No `NEGATIVE CHECK` scaffolding or `.bak`/`.orig` residue in `composeApp/src` (orchestrator re-scanned). **Emulator flag never edited in the tracked file** — Android used `-Pfluxit.firebase.emulator.enabled=true`, iOS used `ORG_GRADLE_PROJECT_...`; `gradle.properties:24` still `false`, orchestrator- and reviewer-confirmed. **Manual two-platform account-switch check (developer-captured, reviewer-inspected, NOT reviewer-executed — see `FB-105-NB3`):** Android driven end-to-end through the real UI with `adb shell input` (`and-fb105-08-full-switch-fixed.log`) shows scope open/close per uid and a NEW `DashboardViewModel created` for B; iOS (`ios-fb105-03-switch-userA-to-userB.log`) shows a genuine two-uid switch `zF1YW6Uk...` -> `YCyhdzOr...`. Accounts were local-emulator-only throwaways; no credentials recorded anywhere. The `1 CHECK(S) FAILED` line in the iOS log is explained setup state (a credential deliberately left signed in by the prepare step), not an `FB-105` regression, and was not filtered out of the evidence. **Not exercised manually:** the `DEC-006` timeout — automated virtual-time coverage only. **`FB-104-NB1` ruled to stay `OPEN`:** narrowed (restoration now awaits a resolved `latestSession` bounded by the same budget, closing the "nothing published yet" half) but provably not closable without an `AuthRepository.restoreSession()` contract change, which the isolation list put out of scope. Reviewer endorsed the developer's reason for not awaiting a *new* emission: both adapters' `distinctUntilChanged` means a confirming restore emits nothing, so such a wait would stall every ordinary cold start for the full 10s. **`FB-101-NB3` addressed honestly:** the new `ListenerInstrumentedAuthRepository` counts real `onStart`/`onCompletion` (the hooks both adapters hang `awaitClose { registration.remove() }` on), so disposal is genuinely proved at the collection level; the test file's own KDoc states plainly that it does NOT prove `FirebaseAuth`/`FIRAuth` itself drops the listener. A previously implicit invariant is now pinned by test: the auth-state listener is **app-scoped by design** and must survive sign-out, or the next sign-in would be invisible. Security: no secrets in any changed file, no Rules file touched, nothing permissive introduced. |

Reused unmodified from `com.fluxit.firebase.list` (module-scoped `internal` visibility): `FirestoreValueCodec`, `CurrentUidProvider`/`FirebaseAuthCurrentUidProvider`, and `FirestoreErrorMapping.kt`'s `toListRepositoryException()`/`ListRepositoryException` (developer's stated rationale: no call site anywhere catches by that class name today, only the neutral `ApplicationError` is consumed cross-platform; flagged for `FB-401` error-taxonomy work to revisit). Added a local generic `Task<T>.awaitTaskResult()` (FB-202's helper is `Task<Void>`-only, untouched). **Counter design:** every counter-affecting mutation (`setCompleted`, `softDeleteItem`, `restoreItem`, `deleteItem`) runs inside `firestore.runTransaction`, reads the item's live `isCompleted`/`deletedAt` first, and only writes a counter delta when it's actually non-zero (idempotent by construction — a repeated or concurrent call reads the already-updated state and computes zero delta); counter scope matches Room's DAO ground truth exactly (`totalItems`=active-only, `completedItems`=active-and-completed-only, read from `Daos.kt:14-15`). `addItem` is an unconditional `totalItems += 1` via one atomic `WriteBatch` (`.set()` new doc + `.update()` counter), justified by the same `DEC-003d-1` creation-only exemption as `createList`. Counter fields are never routed through `FieldPatch` (its constructor `require`s block them) — raw `FieldValue.increment(delta)` only. **Deliberate missing-item asymmetry, flagged for reviewer sign-off:** the four counter-affecting mutations silently no-op if the item doc doesn't exist (mirrors Room's no-match-is-a-no-op `UPDATE ... WHERE` semantics, makes retries after a concurrent hard delete safe); `updateItem`/`setPhotoPath` keep FB-202's existing bare-`.update()`-throws-`NOT_FOUND` behavior. **Chunk strategy for `clearCompleted`:** query-driven loop, `.limit(chunkSize)` (constructor param, default 400, `require`s `chunkSize + 1 <= 500`), one `WriteBatch` per page (item tombstones + trailing counter decrement), loop ends on an empty page; a `MAX_CLEAR_COMPLETED_ITERATIONS = 100_000` guard prevents runaway loops. Evidence: 6 pure JVM boundary tests on the extracted `requireValidClearCompletedChunkSize` function, plus one real-emulator test forcing 3 genuine sequential `WriteBatch.commit()`s (`chunkSize=5`, 14 real items, 5+5+4) that also proves idempotent-retry-on-empty-requery; developer states no test proves the literal 500-document boundary against a live emulator (impractical per the task's own allowance) — flagged as a bounded evidence gap, not silently claimed as full coverage. **Reported commands/results (developer-supplied, not yet reviewer-reproduced):** `testDebugUnitTest`/`testReleaseUnitTest --rerun-tasks` -> 119/119 both (113 FB-202 baseline + 6 new), on-disk JUnit XML mtimes checked; `connectedDebugAndroidTest` scoped to `com.fluxit.firebase.item` against the live Auth (PID 7163/9099) and Firestore-workaround (PID 47151/8080, reused `firebase.fb202-firestore.json`, untouched) emulators and AVD `emulator-5554` -> `OK (20 tests)`, including a genuine offline test (`disableNetwork()`/`enableNetwork()`, ~2s real wall-clock span independently timestamp-checked from logcat), real cross-user denial under the tracked `firestore.rules` (unchanged, items subcollection already covered by the existing owner-only rule), real listener-cancellation, and a 6-concurrent-coroutine `setCompleted(true)` race converging to `completedItems == 1`; commonMain/commonTest Firebase-SDK grep zero hits; `git diff --check` clean; secret-pattern grep found only throwaway `fb204-instrumented-test-key`/`fb204-emulator-only` literals (matches FB-202/FB-203 convention); confirmed no iOS/list-repository/Koin DI/Room/Rules/mobile-config files touched; no commit made. **Other developer-flagged residual risks for reviewer:** `clearCompleted` is per-chunk-atomic not whole-operation-atomic (an item concurrently un-completed/restored mid-sweep could still be counted in that chunk); no floor/clamp on `FieldValue.increment` (a pre-existing-drift scenario could drive a counter negative, no reconciliation job exists yet, presumably Phase 6+); "retry" tests are caller-level (repeated method calls), not SDK-transport-level; cross-user-denial and offline coverage are single-method-representative (`addItem` only), matching FB-202's own precedent. | FB-003-B1 | `FB-003` review, 2026-09-17 | **RESOLVED 2026-09-17** by `DEC-003e-2` (grace period raised 24h -> 30 days, aligning with `DEC-003b`). | `DEC-003e`'s 24h grace period does not cover the app's own offline-write model. Phase 3's mandated safe-replace order is upload bytes to Storage first, then write the referencing Firestore document. If a client completes the upload and then loses connectivity before the second write lands (or that write sits in Firestore's offline queue), the object is unreferenced for longer than 24h and the sweep deletes it. When the client finally reconnects, the queued write lands pointing at a `photoRef` that no longer exists — a broken reference with no cleanup path back. The record's re-check-at-delete-time mitigation does **not** help: at delete time the referencing document still does not exist server-side, so there is nothing to re-check against. Precedent cited by the reviewer: `DEC-003b` chose 30-day tombstone retention *specifically* to "tolerate delayed clients", and the plan names delayed clients as the reason to size retention longer — so 24h is inconsistent with how this codebase already reasons about offline durability. This changes the correct boundary **value**, so it is a policy gap, not an `FB-502`/`FB-503` testing detail. **Returned to the user by the orchestrator as a re-opened sub-decision (`DEC-003e-2`).** | User (re-decision) |

Reviewer ruled this **correct as shipped** (matches the pre-existing `FakeRepositories.kt` convention; reviewer verified no `commonMain` `@Preview` currently references any fake, so nothing is broken). If `FB-104`'s auth UI wants a preview backed by this fake, it must either add a lightweight preview-only fake in `commonMain` or restructure source sets — a planning input for `FB-104`, not a defect in `FB-101`. | `FB-104` |
| FB-101-NB2 | `FB-101` review, 2026-09-18 | OPEN | `AuthError.Unknown` deliberately carries no payload, so a real adapter's diagnostic detail is invisible from `commonMain` and shared tests. | FB-101-NB3 | `FB-101` review, 2026-09-18 | OPEN | **Reviewer's own finding, and the most load-bearing of the three.** `FakeAuthRepository.session` is backed by a plain `MutableStateFlow`, which is always "listening" regardless of collectors. The `cancellingCollectionDoesNotStopTheRepository` test therefore proves only that collector cancellation does not disturb the fake's own state; it **cannot** exercise the interface's stronger documented contract that cancelling the collector releases the underlying listener, because the fake has no underlying listener to release. That contract is real and matters — it is what prevents a leaked Firestore/Auth listener — but it is **unverifiable by construction** at `FB-101`. Not a defect in `FB-101`; an explicit carry-forward so `FB-102`/`FB-103` review does not skip it once a real `callbackFlow`/`awaitClose` (Android) or Swift completion-handler bridge (iOS) exists to cancel. | `FB-102`, `FB-103` |

Local-only — no network round-trip, no live user read or written, nothing reached the Console — but not "no call." **RESOLVED at record time:** the `FB-102` Evidence cell states the precise scope (no auth *operation* touched the default app; one DI wiring-check test constructs it and attaches a local-only listener) rather than the developer's blanket claim. | FB-102-NB2 | `FB-102` review, 2026-09-18 | OPEN | **Informational, owned `FB-104`, not written down anywhere else.** `AndroidAuthRepository.restoreSession()` (`AndroidAuthRepository.kt:74-90`) never calls `gateway.signOut()` on a hard resolution failure, so a genuinely revoked/deleted/expired credential can leave the session permanently in `ResolutionFailed(SessionExpired)` across retries, with **no automatic path back to a clean `SignedOut`**. This is correct against `FB-101`'s documented contract (`ResolutionFailed` = "resolution itself failed, safe to retry") and is **not** a defect in `FB-102`. Reviewer agreed with the developer's own candid self-assessment (stated in the `SessionGateViewModel.kt` KDoc, not hidden) and ruled it non-blocking: both real adapters publish to their internal `MutableStateFlow` synchronously on the same coroutine immediately before returning, so there is **no actual cross-thread hop in the code that exists today** and the risk is speculative against a future adapter change; the worst case is self-correcting (the next emission fixes the gate) and never publishes `Ready` on an unvalidated credential, which is what the acceptance criterion protects. A proper fix requires changing `AuthRepository.restoreSession()` to return its outcome — an **`FB-101` contract change**, deliberately out of `FB-104`'s scope. Must not be reopened as a gate on `FB-104`. **`FB-105` disposition (2026-09-18, reviewer round 1):** narrowed, not closed. No gate-level timeout exists. Neither adapter hung in any run, and both rely on their SDK's own network timeouts. Reviewer ruled deferral acceptable because choosing a timeout duration and a fallback UX is a **product decision, not a correctness fix** — but it is a genuine behavioral trade-off that `FB-105` must pick up deliberately, not inherit silently. If the fallback UX needs deciding, that is a user decision the orchestrator must request. **Resolution (2026-09-18):** the orchestrator obtained the user decision `DEC-006` (10s timeout, fallback to signed-out + non-alarming notice + retry, never an error screen) before delegating `FB-105`; the developer implemented it as `SessionGateViewModel.InitialRestorationTimeout = 10.seconds`, reviewer-verified via `publishGateState` precedence and a passing test proving the fallback holds even when a cached `Authenticated` session was already reported. ## Decision log

Append resolved or superseded decisions; never erase history.

| Date | ID | Status | Decision and rationale | Approved by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-16 | PLAN-001 | ACCEPTED | Use a sequential orchestrator → developer → reviewer → handoff loop with one active implementation task and a canonical tracker. | User request |
| 2026-09-16 | PLAN-002 | ACCEPTED | Establish owner-only baseline Rules in Phase 0; Phase 6 hardens schema validation and completes the security matrix. | Workflow review |
| 2026-09-17 | PLAN-003 | ACCEPTED | A cold-build timing baseline is out of scope for `FB-000`; the ledger and Phase 0 require pass/fail status, not build-time regression tracking. The incremental-duration caveat is disclosed in the baseline document. Optional follow-up: capture cold-build timings at `FB-006`/`FB-007` if Firebase build-time regressions become a concern. | Reviewer assessment |
| 2026-09-17 | DEC-001 | ACCEPTED | Use Firebase **email + password** as the sole sign-in provider. Recovery is Firebase's built-in password-reset email. Rationale: implementable and testable on both platforms with no Apple Developer or Google Cloud OAuth prerequisites, and fully exercisable in the Auth emulator, so Phase 0/1 are not gated on external provisioning. Scope limits: no anonymous auth as primary identity (plan section 2); account **linking is out of scope** while there is exactly one provider, but `AuthRepository` (FB-101) must not foreclose adding Google/Apple later. Adding a provider later is a new decision, not an implementation detail. | User |
| 2026-09-17 | DEC-002a | ACCEPTED | Create a **development Firebase project only** for now; defer all production topology to Phase 7. Rationale: unblocks Phase 0 with minimum external setup. Consequence: `MAN-005` and `FB-707` stay fully open, and the production project, its Rules/index deploys, budget alerts, and backup policy remain undecided. Phase 7 must not assume a production project exists. | User |
| 2026-09-17 | DEC-002b | ACCEPTED | **Gitignore Firebase mobile config for both environments** (`google-services.json`, `GoogleService-Info.plist`). Rationale: most conservative option and safe if the repository ever becomes public. Consequence: a fresh clone cannot build until the developer obtains the config files from the Console, so `FB-005` must add the `.gitignore` entries and `FB-705` must document the retrieval steps in the README. These files are client config, not service-account credentials; security is enforced by Security Rules (Phase 6), never by their absence. | User |
| 2026-09-17 | DEC-002c | ACCEPTED | **Fix the iOS bundle id to `com.fluxit.FluxIt`** by removing the `$(TEAM_ID)` suffix from `PRODUCT_BUNDLE_IDENTIFIER`, so the id is stable regardless of signing team. Rationale: prevents a later `TEAM_ID` change from invalidating an already-registered `GoogleService-Info.plist`. Consequence: requires an Xcode config edit (`Config.xcconfig` / `project.pbxproj`) that must be made and verified **before** `FB-004`/`MAN-001` registers the iOS app. Tracked as new task `FB-012`. | User |
| 2026-09-17 | DEC-003a | ACCEPTED | **Clear the Firebase local persistent cache on sign-out** (terminate + clearPersistence, plus the Auth/Storage equivalents). Rationale: user chose the strongest privacy position for shared devices; it also makes `FB-105`'s user A->B isolation objectively testable rather than assumed. Consequence: the first load after a sign-in refetches from the network, so `FB-404` must present that as initial-loading rather than an error state. | User |
| 2026-09-17 | DEC-003b | ACCEPTED | **Tombstone retention is 30 days.** Rationale: user chose a standard trash-bin window giving real cross-device recovery from accidental deletion. Consequences: this supersedes the current 60-second purge cutoff in `RoomListRepository.purgeExpired`; the 5-second UI undo window is a separate, unchanged concern; tombstones occupy storage for 30 days, so cleanup must actually run on schedule; `FB-201` and Phase 5 must treat 30 days as the eligibility boundary and test the boundary condition. | User |
| 2026-09-17 | DEC-003c | ACCEPTED | **Cleanup is a scheduled Cloud Functions job** sweeping expired tombstones and their Storage objects, cascading list->items->photos server-side. Rationale: user chose the plan's intended design; it is the only option that reliably cascades without trusting a client. **Consequence requiring user action: this requires the Blaze (pay-as-you-go) plan — Cloud Functions and Cloud Scheduler are unavailable on Spark — so `MAN-004`/`FB-506` now genuinely applies and a billing account must be attached before Phase 5.** Cost at this volume is expected to be negligible but is not zero, and the user owns the billing account. Client-side dashboard purge is removed only after backend proof (`FB-504`). | User |

| 2026-09-17 | PLAN-006 | ACCEPTED | Load-bearing constraint from the `FB-005` Storage Rules: the match is **exact-depth with no recursive wildcard**, so `photoRef` must be persisted as exactly `users/{uid}/items/{itemId}/{photoId}` — three fixed segments, and a `photoId` containing `/` is denied. `FB-301` (photoRef rename) and `FB-302` (PhotoStorage contract) must honor this when generating photo identifiers, and must not discover it mid-implementation. | `FB-005` review |
| 2026-09-17 | PLAN-007 | ACCEPTED | Known path asymmetry: Firestore nests items under `users/{uid}/lists/{listId}/items/{itemId}`, but Storage photos live at `users/{uid}/items/{itemId}/{photoId}` with **no `listId` segment**. Consequence: list-cascade deletion (`FB-503`) cannot sweep a Storage prefix by `listId` and must instead enumerate item documents (which do carry `listId`) to discover their `photoRef`s before deleting the list; orphan reconciliation (`FB-302`, `DEC-003`) can only key on `itemId`. Not a Rules defect — recorded now so Phase 3/5 design accounts for it. | `FB-005` review |
| 2026-09-17 | PLAN-004 | ACCEPTED | Evidence rule for all later phases: force every test task whose result is cited, then confirm the JUnit XML timestamp. `BUILD SUCCESSFUL` with an `UP-TO-DATE` test task is not evidence that tests ran. `cleanAllTests` depends only on `cleanIosSimulatorArm64Test` and does not clean either Android/JVM variant. | `FB-000` review |
| 2026-09-17 | PLAN-008 | ACCEPTED | **On iOS, Firebase-touching code must live in Swift, not in `iosMain` Kotlin.** `FB-007` selected Swift Package Manager (`firebase-ios-sdk` `12.19.2`) over the Kotlin CocoaPods integration. The plan's CocoaPods preference is conditional ("**if** it provides the cleanest supported interop"); the reviewer ruled selection of the interop mechanism to be within `FB-007`'s delegated scope ("Select and implement Apple SDK interop"), so this is **not** an unapproved architecture deviation. **Scope of the technical finding, stated precisely:** the reviewer independently confirmed from the resolved package sources that the `FirebaseStorage` SPM target is pure Swift with no public headers, and that the `FirebaseAuth` SPM target explicitly `exclude:`s its `ObjC`/`Public` directories — so via **this** dependency graph, Kotlin/Native cinterop (which consumes only ObjC/C headers) cannot reach a usable Auth/Storage surface. The **broader** claim that no pod wiring could ever work is **partially confirmed only**: `FirebaseAuth.podspec` does bundle public ObjC headers and a deprecated-symbol shim, and no end-to-end CocoaPods+cinterop build was attempted, so the blanket "impossible" framing is not fully proven (see `FB-007-NB2`). **Consequence binding Phase 2 and `FB-008`:** the plan's "temporary platform adapters in `androidMain` and `iosMain`" is satisfiable as written on Android but **not** on iOS. iOS Firebase adapters must be implemented in Swift and bridged back across the framework boundary (e.g. an `expect`/`actual` whose iOS `actual` delegates to Swift), as `FB-007` did for initialization. `FB-008`'s iOS smoke harness and `FB-201`/`FB-301` must be scoped on this basis and must not assume Kotlin-side Firebase access on iOS. | `FB-007` review + orchestrator |
| 2026-09-17 | DEC-003d | ACCEPTED | **Edit conflicts resolve by field-level last-write-wins.** Writes must patch only the fields that actually changed (`update` with a field mask / merge semantics), never replace the whole document, so concurrent edits to *different* fields on the same list or item merge automatically; only a same-field collision resolves to the later writer, silently. Rationale: the user chose this over whole-document LWW (which silently discards the other device's unrelated field changes) and over optimistic concurrency with retry (strongest correctness, but requires conflict-handling UI and error states in every edit path across Phases 2-4 — disproportionate for a single user editing their own lists across their own devices, where true same-field conflicts are rare). Consequences: `FB-201` must define DTO/field mappings so partial updates are expressible and must test that a same-field collision resolves to the later write; `FB-202`-`FB-205` must implement all mutations as field-scoped updates rather than whole-document sets; item counters remain **atomic increments** (`FieldValue.increment`) and are explicitly out of scope for LWW, since increments commute and must not be modeled as a contested field (`FB-204`); `FB-206`'s conflict tests must assert the merge behavior, not just last-writer-wins. **Cross-reference added after `FB-003` review (`FB-003-NB1`):** this interacts with `PLAN-008` — because iOS Firebase access is Swift-side only, the Swift bridge must expose an API surface able to express *which* fields changed (a field mask or changed-keys dictionary), not merely whole-document set operations. `FB-203`/`FB-205` must design that bridging surface up front rather than discovering it mid-implementation, for the same reason `PLAN-006` was recorded ahead of `FB-301`/`FB-302`. Accepted risk: a same-field conflict loses the earlier value with no user-visible notice — and per `FB-003-NB2` this is recorded as a **deliberate "no UI signal" choice**, not an oversight: `FB-401`'s error taxonomy and `FB-404`'s UI states are **not** required to surface a lost-write notification. If Phase 4 later wants one, that is a new decision, not an implementation detail. | User |
| 2026-09-22 | DEC-003d-1 | ACCEPTED | **Clarifying addendum to `DEC-003d`, not a new policy: creating a brand-new document via a fresh, collision-free auto-generated ID is exempt from the field-scoped-`FieldPatch`-only requirement.** `DEC-003d`'s text and rationale are about resolving *concurrent edits to fields on a document that already exists* ("concurrent edits to different fields on the same list or item merge automatically; only a same-field collision resolves to the later writer"). A document created via Firestore's no-argument `.document()` auto-ID has no prior state and no other client can be concurrently mutating a document that does not yet exist, so a `.set()` of its full initial field set is not the whole-document-replace-that-could-clobber-a-concurrent-edit hazard `DEC-003d` was written to prevent — it is equivalent to an atomic create. `FB-201`'s `FieldPatch` type is purpose-built for update-style patches to existing documents (its constructor cannot express document-creation semantics), so routing creation through it would be a type misuse, not a stricter reading. **Raised by:** `FB-202` reviewer round 1 (2026-09-22), ruling on the `AndroidFirebaseListRepository.createList` implementation. Recorded by the orchestrator as a decision-log addendum, not sent back to the user, because it resolves an implementation-reading ambiguity consistent with the original user-approved rationale rather than changing any user-facing tradeoff. **Consequence:** `FB-203`-`FB-205` (iOS list repository, Android/iOS item repositories) may use the same pattern — a `.set()` of the full initial field set on a fresh auto-generated document ID only, at creation time only — without re-litigating this per task. Every other mutation path (update, soft delete, restore, counters) remains field-scoped `FieldPatch`-only per `DEC-003d`, unchanged. | Orchestrator (reviewer-verified reading of already-resolved `DEC-003d`) |
| 2026-09-17 | DEC-003e | ACCEPTED | **Orphaned photos are reconciled by a server-side sweep with a grace period.** The scheduled Cloud Functions job already committed to in `DEC-003c` additionally enumerates each user's Storage objects, compares them against the `photoRef` values held by that user's item documents, and deletes unreferenced objects **older than a grace period** (originally 24h; **superseded by `DEC-003e-2`, now 30 days**). Rationale: the user chose this over client-best-effort-only (which leaks an orphan on every failure path — upload-succeeded-but-document-write-failed, mid-replace termination, offline delete — with nothing ever reclaiming them) and over explicitly accepting leakage (unbounded, billed Storage growth). Reuses infrastructure `DEC-003c` already requires, so it adds no new billing prerequisite beyond the Blaze plan. Consequences: **the grace period is load-bearing** — without it the sweep races in-flight uploads whose owning document has not yet been written, so `FB-502`/`FB-503` must treat it as a correctness requirement and test the boundary, not an optimization; per `PLAN-007` the sweep can only key on `itemId` because Storage photo paths carry no `listId` segment; per `PLAN-006` `photoRef` is exactly `users/{uid}/items/{itemId}/{photoId}`, which is what makes the compare tractable; the sweep must be idempotent and must re-check eligibility at delete time to avoid racing a restore (`FB-502`). | User |
| 2026-09-17 | DEC-003e-2 | ACCEPTED | **The orphan-sweep grace period is 30 days, not 24 hours — superseding that part of `DEC-003e`.** Raised in response to blocking review finding `FB-003-B1`, which showed the 24h window could delete a Storage object whose referencing document was still sitting in a client's offline write queue, leaving a permanently broken `photoRef` with no cleanup path back. Rationale: the user chose alignment with `DEC-003b`'s 30-day tombstone retention, which was itself sized to tolerate delayed clients, over (a) a pending-upload marker document — which closes the hole at any offline duration but costs an extra write per upload, a new document type, and its own marker-orphan cleanup — and over (b) accepting the risk. No new mechanism is introduced; one constant changes. Consequences: `FB-502`/`FB-503` must implement **30 days** as the orphan-eligibility boundary and test that boundary, and the sweep and the tombstone purge now share a single retention constant that must not drift apart; **`FB-502` design note (round-2 review):** the sweep's "referenced" set must enumerate item documents **regardless of tombstone flag** — a soft-deleted item's `photoRef` counts as still referenced for the full tombstone window. If tombstoned documents were excluded from the referenced-set query, a photo could become orphan-eligible on a different clock than its item's 30-day restore window, and a naive implementation could delete a photo the user can still restore. This is distinct from, and additional to, the existing re-check-at-delete-time requirement; genuinely abandoned uploads occupy Storage for 30 days before reclamation, which is accepted. **Residual risk, explicitly accepted:** a client offline for more than 30 days between uploading bytes and its document write landing can still produce a broken reference — the same bound that already applies to tombstone recovery, so the system now has one coherent offline-durability horizon rather than two. | User |
| 2026-09-17 | DEC-004 | ACCEPTED | **`MAN-003` (iOS physical-device signing/access) is permanently `WAIVED`; the iOS target for this project is the simulator.** Rationale given by the user: this is not an App Store-bound project and there is no plan to distribute it or run it on physical iOS hardware, so device signing is not a deferred obligation but a requirement that does not apply. This is a **deliberate scope decision, not an oversight or an unmet gate** — a future session must not reopen it as a blocker. Verified before accepting: the plan's Phase 0 exit criterion requires only that "Android and iOS can authenticate and complete the Firestore/Storage smoke test" (no device clause); the Definition of Done contains no physical-device requirement; and the app's photo flow uses `PHPickerViewController` (photo library), **not** camera capture, which the simulator supports — had it used the camera, this waiver would not have been viable. **Plan line explicitly overridden (not glossed):** the Phase 0 integration-spike list in `FIREBASE_MIGRATION_PLAN.md:76` names "iOS simulator **and physical-device** builds" as a spike target. This waiver **supersedes that line for this project**, on the grounds that it is a spike checklist item rather than a phase exit criterion or a Definition-of-Done clause (neither of which mentions a device), and that the reason for the line — confidence that the app runs on real hardware — does not apply to a project that will not run on real hardware. Recording this explicitly so the decision is not later read as having missed the conflict. Consequences: (1) `FB-011` is satisfied by the waiver plus `FB-007`'s simulator linking/init evidence; (2) `FB-008`'s "two-platform" smoke matrix means Android + **iOS simulator**; (3) `FB-706`'s final matrix is amended from "iOS device" to "iOS simulator"; (4) `FB-405`'s airplane-mode checks are performed by cutting host networking, which approximates but does not reproduce true device airplane mode — recorded as a known fidelity limit, not a pass/fail gap. **Permanently unverified and accepted as such:** code signing, provisioning, embed-and-sign, on-device Keychain token persistence, real device memory/performance behavior, and **real-device background-suspension/termination behavior under memory pressure** (relevant to `FB-405`'s background/foreground and process-restart leg, which is therefore exercised at simulator fidelity only). If this project ever becomes App Store-bound, this decision must be explicitly reopened — the first real-device build would exercise all of the above for the first time. **Open question deliberately NOT decided here:** whether `MAN-005`/`FB-707` production provisioning still applies at all given the project is not shipping; `DEC-002a` already defers production topology to Phase 7, and this is a separate decision for the user when Phase 7 is reached. | User |
| 2026-09-18 | DEC-006 | ACCEPTED | **The session gate times out a hung initial `restoreSession()` after 10 seconds and falls back to the signed-out auth screen with a non-alarming 'could not restore your session' notice plus a retry affordance.** Resolves `FB-104-NB2`, which the `FB-104` reviewer deferred to `FB-105` on the grounds that the duration and fallback UX are a product decision rather than a correctness fix. Rationale given by the user: a bounded spinner that always degrades to a resolved, signed-out state is preferable to an indefinite one, it never displays user data without a resolved UID, and it is consistent with `FB-102-NB2`'s requirement for a 'sign out and retry' recovery affordance rather than retry-only. Consequence: `FB-105` must implement the timeout in `SessionGateViewModel` and cover it with an automated test driving a fake whose `restoreSession()` never completes; the 10s value is a product constant, not a correctness threshold, and may be retuned later without reopening this decision. Rejected alternatives: a 30s window (longer worst-case spinner), a dedicated timeout **error** screen (diverges from `DEC-003a`/`FB-404`'s loading-not-error framing), and deferring again (would leave `FB-104-NB2` open past the phase it was assigned to). | User |

### 2026-09-17 — FB-006 -> FB-007 -> FB-003 session arc, FB-003 reconciliation after reviewer approval (round 3)

- From/to: `firebase_reviewer` (`FB-003` round 3, `APPROVED`, after round 1 `CHANGES_REQUESTED` on blocking finding `FB-003-B1` and round 2 `CHANGES_REQUESTED` on incomplete propagation) → `firebase_handoff` → next session (cold resume, no chat history).

The orchestrator then selected `FB-003`; the user answered `DEC-003d` (field-level last-write-wins) and `DEC-003e` (server-side orphan sweep with a grace period, recorded that round as 24h). Review round 1 raised blocking `FB-003-B1`: the 24h grace period didn't account for the app's own offline-write model — the mandated safe-replace order (upload bytes, then write the referencing document) means a client that goes offline between those two steps can have its object deleted by the sweep before the queued document write ever lands, producing a permanently broken `photoRef`. The orchestrator moved `FB-003` to `BLOCKED_USER` and returned sizing to the user as `DEC-003e-2` with three bounded options (pending-upload marker document; raise the grace period; accept the risk); the user chose to raise it to 30 days, aligning with `DEC-003b`'s tombstone retention. Review round 2 found the fix had not fully propagated: two current-state cells (the Pending decisions `DEC-003` row and the `FB-003` ledger evidence cell) and a Resume Here narrative line still cited 24h or an unresolved `DEC-003e`. Round 3 `APPROVED` after those were fixed, and adopted the reviewer's non-blocking placement suggestion to add two new standing-constraints bullets (`DEC-003e-2` binding `FB-502`/`FB-503`; `DEC-003d` binding `FB-201`-`FB-206`) rather than leaving the constraints implicit in the decision-log prose alone. Three review rounds for one decision task, driven by a genuine correctness gap the codebase's own offline-write model created, not by process friction.
